0Executive summary

Overall risk: HIGH. The release build embeds working backend credentials and SOAP passkeys in plaintext, and allows cleartext HTTP for the primary API host. Any party who downloads the APK can extract these values and call/abuse the shipment and tracking backend.

SMSA Mobile is a logistics app (shipment creation, tracking, address book, payments) built as a single-module Java/Kotlin Android app. The network layer is a hybrid of legacy SOAP/XML (MobileAppService.asmx, WaybillService.svc) and a newer REST API (api/v1/...). Payments use PayFort and STC Pay.

Key findings
  • Critical — hardcoded backend credentials in SOAP request templates: username smsaMobile, password $M$@M0bile, account RU9997, tracking password gf343s$a, plus ~15 service passkey values.
  • High — cleartext HTTP permitted for mobileapi.smsaexpress.com, track.smsaexpress.com, strack.cloudapp.net.
  • Medium — 11 app activities explicitly exported without permission (Login, CreateShipment, …).
  • Medium — a public leaf certificate is bundled as a trust anchor.
  • No certificate pinning; no native libraries.

4Architecture & package map

Single-module app, Java/Kotlin, MVP/MVVM style with Activities as primary UI containers. Networking is split between a SOAP client and Retrofit REST services.

6Network layer & complete API contract

6.2 Authentication model

SOAP calls carry service-level passkey XML elements and (for pickup) a fixed username/password/accountNumber. REST calls use a session/token returned by api/Users/CheckUserLogin; loyalty calls pass an L-API-KEY header.

6.3 Complete Retrofit / service contract

8Secrets & hardcoded credentials

Critical. These are shipped as string constants and are recoverable with apktool/strings in seconds. Rotate all of them and move to per-user tokens.

9TLS / cleartext audit

<network-security-config>
  <base-config cleartextTrafficPermitted="false">
    <trust-anchors>
      <certificates src="system" />
      <certificates src="@raw/usertrust_root" />
    </trust-anchors>
  </base-config>
  <domain-config cleartextTrafficPermitted="true">
    <domain includeSubdomains="false">track.smsaexpress.com</domain>
    <domain includeSubdomains="false">mobileapi.smsaexpress.com</domain>
    <domain includeSubdomains="true">strack.cloudapp.net</domain>
  </domain-config>
  <domain-config cleartextTrafficPermitted="false">
    <domain includeSubdomains="true">smsaexpress.com</domain>
  </domain-config>
</network-security-config>
PropertyResult
Cleartext defaultDisabled (good)
Cleartext for API hostEnabled — mobileapi.smsaexpress.com
Trusts user CAsNo (good)
Bundled CAres/raw/usertrust_root.crt — leaf cert *.smsaexpress.com (Network Solutions RSA OV SSL CA 4), valid 2025-10-20 → 2026-11-20, SHA-256 55E0926F…9D1C
Certificate pinningNone
Note: bundling a public leaf certificate as a trust anchor is not pinning and adds no confidentiality; it only couples the app to that certificate's expiry.

10Security findings

SeverityFindingImpact / remediation
CriticalBackend credentials & SOAP passkeys hardcodedAnyone can extract and call the shipment/tracking backend. Rotate credentials, use per-user auth, remove from client.
HighCleartext HTTP allowed for primary API and tracking hostsSession tokens / PII exposed to on-path attackers. Enforce HTTPS-only.
MediumExported activities without permissionAny app can launch Login/CreateShipment/ShipmentPickUp etc. Set exported=false or protect with signature permission.
MediumLeaf certificate bundled as trust anchorWeak trust design; replace with proper pinning (or none) and system trust.
MediumNo certificate pinningMitM feasible if a CA is trusted. Add pinning for api hosts.
LowGoogle/Firebase API keys in resourcesRestrict keys by package + SHA-1 in Google Cloud console.
InfoallowBackup=falseGood.
InfoNo deep linksReduces external entry points.

11Reconstruction blueprint (agent-ready)

11.1 Recommended stack

  • Language/UI: Kotlin, Jetpack (Navigation, ViewModel, LiveData/Flow), Material 3.
  • Networking: REST via Retrofit + OkHttp (JSON); legacy SOAP via OkHttp + XML serializer (or migrate backend to REST). Gson/Moshi for models.
  • Local data: Room for addresses, shipments cache, session; DataStore for prefs.
  • Maps/location: Google Maps SDK + FusedLocationProvider.
  • Push: Firebase Cloud Messaging (+ Huawei Push Kit for HMS devices).
  • Payments: PayFort SDK + STC Pay merchant API.
  • Images: Glide/Coil.

11.2 Module structure

app/
 ├─ data/
 │   ├─ network/  ApiService (REST), SoapClient (ASMX/SVC), StcPayService
 │   ├─ model/    request/response DTOs (see Appendix A)
 │   ├─ repo/     AuthRepo, ShipmentRepo, TrackingRepo, PaymentRepo
 │   └─ local/    Room entities + DAOs
 ├─ ui/
 │   ├─ onboarding/ (Language, Intro, Login, Register, ForgotPassword, CreatePassword)
 │   ├─ home/       (shipments dashboard, notifications)
 │   ├─ create/     (CreateShipment, addresses, currency, review)
 │   ├─ shipments/  (ShipmentDetails, Pickup, ShipmentCreated)
 │   ├─ address/    (list, add/edit, map selection, validation)
 │   ├─ payments/   (SavedCards, StcPay, PayFort 3DS)
 │   └─ account/    (Biometric, LoyaltyCard, About, Contact)
 └─ util/ (session, validators, location, permission helper)

11.3 Core user flows to implement

  1. Onboarding: language → intro → login/registration → OTP/passcode → home.
  2. Login: POST api/Users/IsExistingUser then POST api/Users/CheckUserLogin; persist token.
  3. Create shipment: pick sender/receiver address (map + validation), weight/currency, rate inquiry (SOAP RateInquiry), confirm → GenerateAWB → ShipmentCreated.
  4. Track: by AWB or mobile via SOAP getTracking/GetShipmentsByPhone.
  5. Pay: PayFort card/3DS or STC Pay PaymentInquiry → MobilePaymentAuthorize.
  6. Account: saved addresses, saved cards, biometric lock, loyalty points.

11.4 Backend contract checklist

Implement the REST paths in §6.3 and the SOAP operations listed under APIService (MobileAppService.asmx) and WaybillService.svc. Every SOAP envelope needs the service passkey; treat these as server-side secrets, not client constants.

V2-0UI & resource inventory overview

This volume documents everything needed to reproduce the user interface, copy and local data layer of SMSA Mobile: design tokens, every layout, every string, menus, navigation, fonts, drawables, manifest specifics, preferences and assets. Class names, API contracts and backend behaviour are in Volume I.

Layout files309 across 4 configuration folders
Colors569
Dimensions1651
Strings1053 (default locale) · plurals 3 · string-arrays 5
Styles / themes1229 defined · 102 referenced by layouts
Drawables726 unique (animated-vector: 22, bitmap: 83, layer-list: 10, ripple: 7, selector: 26, shape: 154, transition: 6, vector: 317, xml: 101)
Fonts11 font resources
Menus1 · navigation graphs 1
Animations50 anim XML · raw resources 7
Assets13 entries
Screen → layout mappings67 classes resolved
Local persistenceDataStore classes 1 · SharedPreferences holders 10 · Room databases 0
How to use this volume. Layout trees show the exact view hierarchy, ids, literal texts, sizes and orientations. Design values (colors/dimens/styles) are given in full, so a rebuilt layout will look identical. Exact attribute-level XML and binary assets are additionally shipped in the companion archive resources/smsa_mobile_res.zip (see §V2-9).
No @Composable functions and no Room databases were found in the app code: the UI is classic Android Views + XML layouts (Material Components / AppCompat), and persistence uses DataStore/SharedPreferences plus the network cache. Any library-bundled Compose runtime is unused by app screens.

V2-8UI reconstruction blueprint

8.1 Screen assembly order

  1. Create the design system from §V2-1 (colors → colors.xml/theme, dimens, text styles).
  2. For every screen in §V2-2, create the listed layout(s) from §V2-3 using the same view classes, ids, orientation and sizes. Bind with ViewBinding exactly as the original does.
  3. Fill every label from §V2-4. Use the same keys so localisation files can be dropped in.
  4. Wire menus (§V2-5) and navigation (§V2-5/§V2-2) with the same ids.
  5. Apply manifest flags from §V2-6 (theme, cleartext, backup, queries, deep links).
  6. Implement the data layer from §V2-7 and connect to the API contract in Volume I §6.

8.2 Fidelity checklist

8.3 What still comes from the archive / APK

Binary images, vector path data, nine-patch chunks, font binaries, localised values-*, and exact XML attributes are shipped in the companion archive. This is deliberate: reproducing them textually in a PDF would be lossy and enormous.

V2-9Companion resource archive

Exact, loss-free UI inputs are packaged next to this report:

resources/smsa_mobile_res.zip
 ├─ res/layout*/              all layout XML (every configuration)
 ├─ res/drawable*/            vectors, shapes, selectors, bitmaps, nine-patch
 ├─ res/values*/              colors, dimens, styles, strings, arrays, plurals (all locales)
 ├─ res/menu/ res/navigation/ res/anim/ res/animator/ res/font/ res/raw/
 ├─ res/mipmap*/              launcher icons
 ├─ AndroidManifest.xml       decoded manifest
 └─ assets/                   bundled assets

Generated with apktool d -r from the APK whose SHA-256 is recorded on the cover. Import it directly into an Android project; then apply the API/data contracts from Volume I.
